**Summary for migration impact analysis:**

## TEST SURFACE MAPPING: Migration to `artagents/packs/<pack>/<name>/` and Qualified IDs

**Total: 80 test files**

### BREAKDOWN:

**38 tests will need code changes** (import direct `run` modules):
- Direct imports like `from artagents.executors.<name> import run as <func>`
- Examples: test_arrange.py, test_cut_timeline_resume.py, test_mixed_mode_cut.py, test_refine.py, etc.
- These will need updated imports to use new pack structure or registry-based loading

**6 tests will definitely break** (hardcoded filesystem layout):
1. **test_default_registry_scopes.py** - Asserts metadata paths end with `artagents/executors/<folder>` (lines 29-41)
2. **test_doctor_setup.py** - Expects error messages containing "artagents/orchestrators/vibecomfy missing orchestrator.yaml" (line 108)
3. **test_executor_move_wrappers.py** - Dynamically imports `artagents.executors.<step_name>.run` (lines 22, 30)
4. **test_elements_cli.py** - Asserts `.artagents/elements/effects-text-card` path (line 62)
5. **test_elements_install.py** - Hardcoded venv paths `.artagents/executors/vibecomfy/venv` (lines 37-38)
6. **test_mixed_mode_cut.py** - Reads source from `artagents/executors/cut/run.py` (line 97)
7. **test_onboarding_docs.py** - Validates docs contain canonical paths like `artagents/executors/<slug>/` (lines 49-51)
8. **test_threads_producer_optins.py** - Globs `(repo / "artagents" / "executors").glob("*/run.py")` (line 52)

**12 threads tests (new)** mostly safe IF qualified IDs work through registry API:
- These import from `artagents.executors.registry`, `artagents.executors.runner`, `artagents.orchestrators.runner`
- They reference executor/orchestrator by ID in test data, NOT by filesystem path
- Will survive if qualified IDs (e.g., `builtin.cut`, `external.moirae`) work through the registry

**22 other tests** likely safe (business logic, data flow, pipeline tests with no direct executor/orchestrator layout coupling)

**2 registry/schema API tests** are fully safe (no layout dependencies)

---

### COST ESTIMATE:

- **Critical fixes: 8 tests** - hardcoded paths and glob patterns (1-2 hours)
- **Moderate updates: 38 tests** - import statements (1-2 hours with bulk replacement, assuming pack namespace resolves)
- **Threads tests: 12** - No changes if `qualified_ids` work through registry (0 hours if registry migration is solid)
- **Total estimated effort: 2-4 hours of test updates + risk of registry API breakage**

The migration is feasible; biggest risk is whether the registry API correctly resolves qualified IDs (e.g., does `registry.get("builtin.cut")` work after moving the source file to `artagents/packs/builtin/cut/`).

```
18	def executor_argv(script_name: str, python_exec: str) -> list[str]:
19	    """Return argv tokens that invoke an executor's canonical module entrypoint.
20	
21	    `script_name` accepts either a bare step name (``"transcribe"``) or the
22	    legacy ``bin/`` filename (``"transcribe.py"``).
23	    """
24	    stem = script_name[:-3] if script_name.endswith(".py") else script_name
25	    module = _EXECUTOR_MODULE_OVERRIDES.get(stem, f"artagents.executors.{stem}.run")
26	    return [python_exec, "-m", module]
```

Repo: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents

We are doing a clean-cut migration to a new "packs" layout (no back-compat shims, no fallback paths, no deprecated names supported). Given that, your job is to identify EVERYTHING in the existing codebase that is itself a compatibility shim, alias layer, fallback, or vestigial path that we should DELETE during the migration rather than carry forward.

Look for:
1. **Bare-name id resolution.** `artagents/executors/registry.py` and `artagents/orchestrators/registry.py` likely auto-prefix bare names with `builtin.` somewhere. Find every place that maps `cut` → `builtin.cut` or similar. Under no-shim, these go away — callers must use qualified ids.
2. **Aliasing layers.** `artagents/executors/understand/run.py:176` has `_resolve_alias()`. Find any other `_resolve_alias`, `_legacy_`, `_deprecated_`, `_compat`, `_fallback`, `_old_` etc. in executor/orchestrator/element code. Are they migration-era debt or load-bearing?
3. **Skipped/special-cased dir names.** `artagents/executors/registry.py:177` has a skipped set `{"__pycache__", "actions", "builtin", "bundled", "curated"}`. What did those names mean historically? Are `actions` and `curated` still in use? Empty `bundled/` dirs were noted — are they vestigial?
4. **Module override maps.** `artagents/_paths.py` hardcodes `"artagents.executors.render.run"` as a module override. Why does this exist? Is it a workaround for an old layout? Does the migration remove the need entirely?
5. **Multiple discovery roots that do the same job.** `_bundled_folder_roots`, `_builtin_folder_roots`, `_project_folder_roots` — are these three roots really three distinct concepts or are some just legacy?
6. **Comments flagging compat/legacy.** Grep the whole `artagents/` tree for: `legacy`, `deprecated`, `back.?compat`, `compat`, `TODO.*remove`, `XXX.*remove`, `for backwards`, `for backward`, `historical`, `old layout`, `pre-migration`. List file:line + comment text.
7. **Empty `__init__.py` placeholder packages, empty `bundled/` dirs.** What's there only to satisfy old imports?
8. **`isolation.requirements` vs `pyproject.toml` vs `requirements.txt`.** install.py supports all three. Are all three still needed or is one a legacy path?
9. **The `package_id` metadata field.** Used to group executors under one venv. Under packs, `pack_id` becomes the natural key — does `package_id` get renamed or removed entirely?
10. **CLI subcommand aliases.** Any `add_parser` calls with `aliases=[...]` for renamed commands? Any deprecated flags?
11. **Cross-kind back-references.** orchestrator/registry.py importing executor/registry.py is structural, not compat — but are there any backref imports that exist only because something used to live elsewhere?
12. **String-literal ids like `"builtin.cut"` in code that could just be derived from the new layout.** Hardcoded ids that were workarounds for missing discovery.

For each item found, answer: **purge it** (delete), **rename it** (carry forward with a new name), or **keep it** (still load-bearing, not actually compat). One-line rationale.

Report under 700 words, organized by the 12 categories above. The output is a kill list for a clean-cut migration. file_path:line_number references throughout. Don't propose how to do the migration — just identify what to delete or rename.

```
11	from .banodoco_catalog import BanodocoCatalogConfig, load_banodoco_catalog_executors
12	from .folder import load_folder_executors
13	from .schema import ExecutorDefinition, ExecutorValidationError, load_executor_manifest, validate_executor_definition
14	
15	
16	BUNDLED_PACKAGE = "artagents.executors.bundled"
17	CURATED_PACKAGE = "artagents.executors.curated"
18	BUILTIN_STEP_ORDER: tuple[str, ...] = (
19	    "transcribe",
20	    "scenes",
21	    "quality_zones",

37	class ExecutorRegistryError(ExecutorValidationError):
38	    """Raised when a executor registry is inconsistent."""
39	
40	
41	class ExecutorRegistry:
42	    """Small in-memory registry keyed by executor id."""
43	
44	    def __init__(self, executors: Iterable[ExecutorDefinition | dict[str, Any]] = ()) -> None:
45	        self._executors: dict[str, ExecutorDefinition] = {}
46	        for executor in executors:
47	            self.register(executor)
48	
49	    def register(self, executor: ExecutorDefinition | dict[str, Any]) -> ExecutorDefinition:
50	        definition = validate_executor_definition(executor)
51	        if definition.id in self._executors:
52	            raise ExecutorRegistryError(f"duplicate executor id {definition.id!r}")
53	        self._executors[definition.id] = definition
54	        return definition
55	
56	    def get(self, executor_id: str) -> ExecutorDefinition:
57	        try:
58	            return self._executors[executor_id]
59	        except KeyError as exc:
60	            raise KeyError(f"unknown executor id {executor_id!r}") from exc
61	
62	    def list(self, kind: str | None = None) -> tuple[ExecutorDefinition, ...]:
63	        if kind is not None and kind not in {"built_in", "external"}:
64	            raise ExecutorRegistryError("kind must be one of ['built_in', 'external']")
65	        executors = self._executors.values()
66	        if kind is not None:
67	            executors = [executor for executor in executors if executor.kind == kind]
68	        return tuple(sorted(executors, key=lambda executor: executor.id))
69	
70	    def validate_all(self) -> tuple[ExecutorDefinition, ...]:
71	        for executor in self._executors.values():
72	            validate_executor_definition(executor)
73	        self._validate_graph_references()
74	        return self.list()
75	
76	    def to_dict(self, kind: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
77	        return {"executors": [executor.to_dict() for executor in self.list(kind=kind)]}
78	
79	    def to_json(self, *, kind: str | None = None, indent: int | None = 2) -> str:
80	        return json.dumps(self.to_dict(kind=kind), indent=indent, sort_keys=True)
81	
82	    def as_mapping(self) -> MappingProxyType[str, ExecutorDefinition]:
83	        return MappingProxyType(dict(self._executors))
84	
85	    def _validate_graph_references(self) -> None:
86	        known_ids = set(self._executors)
87	        for executor in self._executors.values():
88	            for dependency in executor.graph.depends_on:
89	                if dependency not in known_ids:
90	                    raise ExecutorRegistryError(f"executor {executor.id!r} depends on unknown executor {dependency!r}")
91	                if dependency == executor.id:
92	                    raise ExecutorRegistryError(f"executor {executor.id!r} cannot depend on itself")
93	
94	
95	def executor_id_for_step(step_name: str) -> str:
96	    return f"builtin.{step_name}"
97	
98	
99	def load_builtin_executors() -> tuple[ExecutorDefinition, ...]:
100	    folder_executors: list[ExecutorDefinition] = []
101	    expected_ids = {executor_id_for_step(step_name) for step_name in BUILTIN_STEP_ORDER}
102	    for path in _builtin_folder_roots():
103	        folder_executors.extend(load_folder_executors(path))
104	    by_id = {executor.id: executor for executor in folder_executors if executor.id in expected_ids}
105	    missing = [executor_id_for_step(step_name) for step_name in BUILTIN_STEP_ORDER if executor_id_for_step(step_name) not in by_id]
106	    if missing:
107	        raise ExecutorRegistryError(f"missing built-in executor folder metadata: {', '.join(missing)}")
108	    return tuple(by_id[executor_id_for_step(step_name)] for step_name in BUILTIN_STEP_ORDER)
109	
110	
111	def load_curated_executors() -> tuple[ExecutorDefinition, ...]:
112	    executors: list[ExecutorDefinition] = []
113	    for path in _curated_manifest_paths():
114	        executors.append(load_executor_manifest(path))
115	    for path in _curated_folder_roots():
116	        executors.extend(load_folder_executors(path))
117	    return tuple(executors)

124	    for path in _bundled_folder_roots():
125	        executors.extend(load_folder_executors(path))

129	def load_default_registry(banodoco_config: BanodocoCatalogConfig | None = None) -> ExecutorRegistry:
130	    registry = ExecutorRegistry()
131	    for executor in load_builtin_executors():
132	        registry.register(executor)
133	    for executor in load_bundled_executors():
134	        registry.register(executor)
135	    for executor in load_curated_executors():
136	        registry.register(executor)
137	    for executor in load_project_executors():
138	        if executor.id not in registry.as_mapping():
139	            registry.register(executor)
140	    if banodoco_config is not None and banodoco_config.enabled:
141	        for executor in load_banodoco_catalog_executors(banodoco_config):
142	            registry.register(executor)
143	    registry.validate_all()
144	    return registry
```

```
26	class OrchestratorRegistryError(OrchestratorValidationError):
27	    """Raised when an orchestrator registry is inconsistent."""
28	
29	
30	class OrchestratorRegistry:
31	    """Small in-memory registry keyed by orchestrator id."""
32	
33	    def __init__(
34	        self,
35	        orchestrators: Iterable[OrchestratorDefinition | dict[str, Any]] = (),
36	        *,
37	        executor_registry: ExecutorRegistry | None = None,
38	    ) -> None:
39	        self._orchestrators: dict[str, OrchestratorDefinition] = {}
40	        self._executor_registry = executor_registry
41	        for orchestrator in orchestrators:
42	            self.register(orchestrator)
43	
44	    def register(self, orchestrator: OrchestratorDefinition | dict[str, Any]) -> OrchestratorDefinition:
45	        definition = validate_orchestrator_definition(orchestrator)
46	        if definition.id in self._orchestrators:
47	            raise OrchestratorRegistryError(f"duplicate orchestrator id {definition.id!r}")
48	        self._orchestrators[definition.id] = definition
49	        return definition
50	
51	    def get(self, orchestrator_id: str) -> OrchestratorDefinition:
52	        try:
53	            return self._orchestrators[orchestrator_id]
54	        except KeyError as exc:
55	            raise KeyError(f"unknown orchestrator id {orchestrator_id!r}") from exc
56	
57	    def list(self, kind: str | None = None) -> tuple[OrchestratorDefinition, ...]:
58	        if kind is not None and kind not in {"built_in", "external"}:
59	            raise OrchestratorRegistryError("kind must be one of ['built_in', 'external']")
60	        orchestrators: Iterable[OrchestratorDefinition] = self._orchestrators.values()
61	        if kind is not None:
62	            orchestrators = [orchestrator for orchestrator in orchestrators if orchestrator.kind == kind]
63	        return tuple(sorted(orchestrators, key=lambda orchestrator: orchestrator.id))
64	
65	    def validate_all(
66	        self,
67	        *,
68	        executor_registry: ExecutorRegistry | None = None,
69	    ) -> tuple[OrchestratorDefinition, ...]:
70	        for orchestrator in self._orchestrators.values():
71	            validate_orchestrator_definition(orchestrator)
72	        self._validate_child_executors(executor_registry=executor_registry)
73	        self._validate_child_orchestrators()
74	        return self.list()
75	
76	    def to_dict(self, kind: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
77	        return {"orchestrators": [orchestrator.to_dict() for orchestrator in self.list(kind=kind)]}
78	
79	    def to_json(self, *, kind: str | None = None, indent: int | None = 2) -> str:
80	        return json.dumps(self.to_dict(kind=kind), indent=indent, sort_keys=True)
81	
82	    def as_mapping(self) -> MappingProxyType[str, OrchestratorDefinition]:
83	        return MappingProxyType(dict(self._orchestrators))
84	
85	    def _validate_child_executors(self, *, executor_registry: ExecutorRegistry | None) -> None:
86	        registry = executor_registry or self._executor_registry or load_default_executor_registry()
87	        known_executor_ids = set(registry.as_mapping())
88	        for orchestrator in self._orchestrators.values():
89	            for child_executor in orchestrator.child_executors:
90	                if child_executor not in known_executor_ids:
91	                    raise OrchestratorRegistryError(
92	                        f"orchestrator {orchestrator.id!r} references unknown child executor {child_executor!r}"
93	                    )
94	
95	    def _validate_child_orchestrators(self) -> None:
96	        known_orchestrator_ids = set(self._orchestrators)
97	        graph: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {}
98	        for orchestrator in self._orchestrators.values():
99	            children: list[str] = []
100	            for child_orchestrator in orchestrator.child_orchestrators:
101	                if child_orchestrator not in known_orchestrator_ids:
102	                    raise OrchestratorRegistryError(
103	                        f"orchestrator {orchestrator.id!r} references unknown child orchestrator {child_orchestrator!r}"
104	                    )
105	                if child_orchestrator == orchestrator.id:
106	                    raise OrchestratorRegistryError(f"orchestrator {orchestrator.id!r} cannot reference itself")
107	                children.append(child_orchestrator)
108	            graph[orchestrator.id] = tuple(children)
109	        self._validate_no_cycles(graph)
110	
111	    def _validate_no_cycles(self, graph: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]]) -> None:
112	        visiting: set[str] = set()
113	        visited: set[str] = set()
114	        stack: list[str] = []
115	
116	        def visit(orchestrator_id: str) -> None:
117	            if orchestrator_id in visited:
118	                return
119	            if orchestrator_id in visiting:
120	                cycle = stack[stack.index(orchestrator_id) :] + [orchestrator_id]
121	                raise OrchestratorRegistryError(f"orchestrator cycle detected: {' -> '.join(cycle)}")
122	            visiting.add(orchestrator_id)
123	            stack.append(orchestrator_id)
124	            for child in graph.get(orchestrator_id, ()):
125	                visit(child)
126	            stack.pop()
127	            visiting.remove(orchestrator_id)
128	            visited.add(orchestrator_id)
129	
130	        for orchestrator_id in sorted(graph):
131	            visit(orchestrator_id)
132
```

```
1	from __future__ import annotations
2	
3	from pathlib import Path
4	
5	PACKAGE_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
6	REPO_ROOT = PACKAGE_ROOT.parent
7	WORKSPACE_ROOT = REPO_ROOT.parent
8	
9	# Map of bin-style script filenames (e.g. "render_remotion.py") to the
10	# canonical executor module that owns the behavior. Keeps callers free to use
11	# the legacy filename while we migrate, and makes the one name mismatch
12	# (render_remotion -> executors.render) explicit in one place.
13	_EXECUTOR_MODULE_OVERRIDES = {
14	    "render_remotion": "artagents.executors.render.run",
15	}
16	
17	
18	def executor_argv(script_name: str, python_exec: str) -> list[str]:
19	    """Return argv tokens that invoke an executor's canonical module entrypoint.
20	
21	    `script_name` accepts either a bare step name (``"transcribe"``) or the
22	    legacy ``bin/`` filename (``"transcribe.py"``).
23	    """
24	    stem = script_name[:-3] if script_name.endswith(".py") else script_name
25	    module = _EXECUTOR_MODULE_OVERRIDES.get(stem, f"artagents.executors.{stem}.run")
26	    return [python_exec, "-m", module]
27
```

```
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Unified dispatcher for ArtAgents understanding executors.
3	
4	`builtin.understand` selects an underlying modality executor (audio, visual,
5	or video) via `--mode` and forwards the remaining argv unchanged. This is
6	deliberately a thin executor — not an orchestrator — because it wraps exactly
7	one executor call with a switch.
8	"""
9	
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	import argparse
13	from collections.abc import Callable
14	from importlib import import_module
15	import sys
16	
17	
18	ALIASES: dict[str, str | Callable[[list[str]], int]] = {
19	    "audio": "artagents.executors.audio_understand.run:main",
20	    "image": "artagents.executors.visual_understand.run:main",
21	    "visual": "artagents.executors.visual_understand.run:main",
22	    "video": "artagents.executors.video_understand.run:main",
23	}
24	
25	
26	def build_parser() -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
27	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
28	        description="Dispatch to audio, image/visual, or video understanding executors.",
29	        epilog=(
30	            "Examples: understand --mode image --image frame.jpg --query 'What is here?'; "
31	            "understand --mode audio --audio quote.wav; "
32	            "understand --mode video --video source.mp4 --at 01:20"
33	        ),
34	    )
35	    parser.add_argument(
36	        "--mode",
37	        choices=sorted(ALIASES),
38	        required=True,
39	        help="Understanding modality. All other arguments are forwarded to the selected executor.",
40	    )
41	    return parser
42	
43	
44	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
45	    args, forwarded = build_parser().parse_known_args(argv)
46	    return _resolve_alias(args.mode)(forwarded)
47	
48	
49	def _resolve_alias(mode: str) -> Callable[[list[str]], int]:
50	    target = ALIASES[mode]
51	    if callable(target):
52	        return target
53	    module_name, function_name = target.split(":", 1)
54	    return getattr(import_module(module_name), function_name)
55	
56	
57	if __name__ == "__main__":
58	    raise SystemExit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
59
```

```
1	"""Repository structure guardrails for ArtAgents canonical concepts."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	from dataclasses import dataclass
6	from pathlib import Path
7	
8	from artagents._paths import REPO_ROOT
9	from artagents.executors.folder import load_folder_executors
10	from artagents.orchestrators.folder import load_folder_orchestrators
11	
12	
13	LEGACY_PUBLIC_DIRS = ("conductors", "performers", "instruments", "primitives")
14	LEGACY_LOCAL_DIRS = ("performers", "conductors", "nodes", "instruments", "primitives")
15	INTERNAL_EXECUTOR_DIRS = {"__pycache__", "actions", "builtin", "bundled", "curated"}
16	INTERNAL_ORCHESTRATOR_DIRS = {"__pycache__", "bundled", "curated"}
17	TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_FILES = {
18	    "__init__.py",
19	    "__main__.py",
20	    "_paths.py",
21	    "doctor.py",
22	    "pipeline.py",
23	    "setup_cli.py",
24	    "structure.py",
25	    "theme_schema.py",
26	    "timeline.py",
27	}
28	TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS = {
29	    "__pycache__",
30	    "audit",
31	    "contracts",
32	    "domains",
33	    "elements",
34	    "executors",
35	    "modalities",
36	    "orchestrators",
37	    "threads",
38	    "utilities",
39	}
40	
41	
42	@dataclass(frozen=True)
43	class StructureReport:
44	    errors: tuple[str, ...]
45	
46	    @property
47	    def ok(self) -> bool:
48	        return not self.errors
49	
50	
51	def validate_repo_structure(root: str | Path = REPO_ROOT) -> StructureReport:
52	    repo_root = Path(root)
53	    errors: list[str] = []
54	    errors.extend(_validate_legacy_dirs(repo_root))
55	    errors.extend(_validate_local_state_dirs(repo_root))
56	    errors.extend(_validate_top_level_artagents(repo_root / "artagents"))
57	    errors.extend(_validate_executor_folders(repo_root / "artagents" / "executors"))
58	    errors.extend(_validate_orchestrator_folders(repo_root / "artagents" / "orchestrators"))
59	    return StructureReport(errors=tuple(errors))
60	
61	
62	def _validate_legacy_dirs(repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:
63	    errors: list[str] = []
64	    for dirname in LEGACY_PUBLIC_DIRS:
65	        candidate = repo_root / "artagents" / dirname
66	        if candidate.exists():
67	            errors.append(f"legacy public package must not exist: {candidate.relative_to(repo_root)}")
68	    return errors
69	
70	
71	def _validate_local_state_dirs(repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:
72	    errors: list[str] = []
73	    local_root = repo_root / ".artagents"
74	    if not local_root.exists():
75	        return errors
76	    for dirname in LEGACY_LOCAL_DIRS:
77	        candidate = local_root / dirname
78	        if candidate.exists():
79	            errors.append(f"legacy local state directory must not exist: {candidate.relative_to(repo_root)}")
80	    return errors
81	
82	
83	def _validate_top_level_artagents(package_root: Path) -> list[str]:
84	    errors: list[str] = []
85	    for child in sorted(package_root.iterdir()):
86	        if child.name.startswith("."):
87	            continue
88	        if child.is_file() and child.suffix == ".py" and child.name not in TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_FILES:
89	            errors.append(f"top-level artagents module must move to a canonical package: {child.relative_to(package_root.parents[0])}")
90	        if child.is_dir() and child.name not in TOP_LEVEL_ARTAGENTS_DIRS:
91	            errors.append(f"top-level artagents directory is not a canonical concept: {child.relative_to(package_root.parents[0])}")
92	    return errors
93	
94	
95	def _validate_executor_folders(executors_root: Path) -> list[str]:
96	    if not executors_root.is_dir():
97	        return [f"missing executors directory: {executors_root}"]
98	
99	    errors: list[str] = []
100	    for folder in _public_child_dirs(executors_root, INTERNAL_EXECUTOR_DIRS):
101	        errors.extend(_require_files(folder, ("executor.yaml", "run.py", "STAGE.md"), root=executors_root.parents[1]))
102	        if _has_any(folder, ("orchestrator.yaml", "orchestrator.yml", "orchestrator.json", "orchestrator.py")):
103	            errors.append(f"executor folder contains orchestrator metadata: {folder.relative_to(executors_root.parents[1])}")
104	        try:
105	            definitions = load_folder_executors(folder)
106	        except Exception as exc:
107	            errors.append(f"invalid executor folder {folder.relative_to(executors_root.parents[1])}: {exc}")
108	            continue
109	        if not definitions:
110	            errors.append(f"executor folder emitted no executor metadata: {folder.relative_to(executors_root.parents[1])}")
111	            continue
112	        for definition in definitions:
113	            if definition.kind == "built_in" and definition.id.startswith("builtin."):
114	                expected = f"builtin.{folder.name}"
115	                if definition.id != expected:
116	                    errors.append(f"built-in executor {definition.id!r} must live in artagents/executors/{definition.id.removeprefix('builtin.')}")
117	            elif definition.kind == "external" and definition.id.startswith("external."):
118	                package = definition.metadata.get("package_id") or definition.id.removeprefix("external.").split(".", 1)[0]
119	                if package != folder.name:
120	                    errors.append(f"external executor {definition.id!r} package {package!r} must live in artagents/executors/{package}")
121	    return errors
122	
123	
124	def _validate_orchestrator_folders(orchestrators_root: Path) -> list[str]:
125	    if not orchestrators_root.is_dir():
126	        return [f"missing orchestrators directory: {orchestrators_root}"]
127	
128	    errors: list[str] = []
129	    for folder in _public_child_dirs(orchestrators_root, INTERNAL_ORCHESTRATOR_DIRS):
130	        errors.extend(_require_files(folder, ("orchestrator.yaml", "run.py", "STAGE.md"), root=orchestrators_root.parents[1]))
131	        if _has_any(folder, ("executor.yaml", "executor.yml", "executor.json", "executor.py")):
132	            errors.append(f"orchestrator folder contains executor metadata: {folder.relative_to(orchestrators_root.parents[1])}")
133	        try:
134	            definitions = load_folder_orchestrators(folder)
135	        except Exception as exc:
136	            errors.append(f"invalid orchestrator folder {folder.relative_to(orchestrators_root.parents[1])}: {exc}")
137	            continue
138	        if not definitions:
139	            errors.append(f"orchestrator folder emitted no orchestrator metadata: {folder.relative_to(orchestrators_root.parents[1])}")
140	            continue
141	        for definition in definitions:
142	            expected = f"builtin.{folder.name}"
143	            if definition.kind != "built_in" or definition.id != expected:
144	                errors.append(f"built-in orchestrator folder {folder.name!r} must expose id {expected!r}")
145	    return errors
146	
147	
148	def _public_child_dirs(root: Path, skipped: set[str]) -> tuple[Path, ...]:
149	    return tuple(sorted(path for path in root.iterdir() if path.is_dir() and path.name not in skipped and not path.name.startswith(".")))
150	
151	
152	def _require_files(folder: Path, filenames: tuple[str, ...], *, root: Path) -> list[str]:
153	    return [f"{folder.relative_to(root)} missing {filename}" for filename in filenames if not (folder / filename).is_file()]
154	
155	
156	def _has_any(folder: Path, filenames: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
157	    return any((folder / filename).exists() for filename in filenames)
158	
159	
160	__all__ = ["StructureReport", "validate_repo_structure"]
161
```

```
83	def _resolve_theme_arg(value: object) -> Path:
84	    """Resolve --theme as either a theme.json path, a theme directory, or a workspace theme slug."""
85	    candidate = Path(str(value)).expanduser()

88	    if candidate.exists():

90	            return (candidate / "theme.json").resolve()
91	        return candidate.resolve()

201	
202	def load_config(path_text: str) -> dict:
203	    path = Path(path_text).expanduser().resolve()
204	    if not path.is_file():
205	        usage_error(f"artagents: config file not found: {path}")
206	    suffix = path.suffix.lower()
207	    text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
208	    if suffix == ".json":
209	        try:
210	            data = json.loads(text)
211	        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
212	            usage_error(f"artagents: invalid JSON config {path}: {exc.msg}")
213	    elif suffix in {".yaml", ".yml"}:
214	        if yaml is None:
215	            usage_error(f"artagents: YAML config requires PyYAML: {path}")
216	        try:
217	            data = yaml.safe_load(text)
218	        except Exception as exc:
219	            usage_error(f"artagents: invalid YAML config {path}: {exc}")
220	    else:
221	        try:
222	            data = json.loads(text)
223	        except json.JSONDecodeError:
224	            if yaml is None:
225	                usage_error(f"artagents: unsupported config format for {path}; use JSON or install PyYAML for YAML")
226	            try:
227	                data = yaml.safe_load(text)
228	            except Exception as exc:
229	                usage_error(f"artagents: invalid config {path}: {exc}")
230	    if data is None:
231	        return {}
232	    if not isinstance(data, dict):
233	        usage_error(f"artagents: config must decode to an object: {path}")
234	    return data
235	
236	
237	def normalize_config(raw: dict) -> dict:
238	    data = dict(raw)
239	    if "from" in data and "from_step" not in data:
240	        data["from_step"] = data.pop("from")
241	    if "python" in data and "python_exec" not in data:
242	        data["python_exec"] = data.pop("python")
243	    if "assets" in data and "asset" not in data:
244	        data["asset"] = data.pop("assets")
245	    return data
246	
247	
248	def parse_asset_entry(raw: str) -> tuple[str, Path | str]:
249	    if "=" not in raw:
250	        usage_error(f"artagents: invalid --asset value {raw!r}; expected KEY=PATH")
251	    key, path_text = raw.split("=", 1)
252	    key = key.strip()
253	    path_text = path_text.strip()
254	    if not key or not path_text:
255	        usage_error(f"artagents: invalid --asset value {raw!r}; expected KEY=PATH")
256	    if key == "main":
257	        usage_error("artagents: asset key 'main' is reserved; pass the primary video via --video")
258	    if asset_cache.is_url(path_text):
259	        return key, path_text
260	    path = Path(path_text).expanduser().resolve()
261	    if not path.exists():
262	        usage_error(f"artagents: asset path not found for {key!r}: {path}")
263	    return key, path
264	
265	
266	def normalize_many(raw: object, *, key_name: str) -> list[str]:
267	    if raw is None:
268	        return []
269	    if isinstance(raw, str):
270	        return [raw]
271	    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not all(isinstance(item, str) for item in raw):
272	        usage_error(f"artagents: {key_name} must be a string or list of strings")
273	    return list(raw)
274	
275	
276	def normalize_extra_args(raw: object) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
277	    if raw is None:
278	        return {}
279	    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
280	        usage_error("artagents: extra_args must be an object keyed by step name")
281	    allowed_steps = set(STEP_ORDER)
282	    extra_args: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
283	    for step_name, values in raw.items():
284	        if step_name not in allowed_steps:
285	            usage_error(f"artagents: unknown extra_args step {step_name!r}")
286	        if not isinstance(values, list) or not all(isinstance(item, (str, int, float)) for item in values):
287	            usage_error(f"artagents: extra_args[{step_name!r}] must be a list of CLI tokens")
288	        extra_args[step_name] = [str(item) for item in values]
289	    return extra_args
290	
291	
292	def resolve_args(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> argparse.Namespace:
293	    parsed = build_parser().parse_args(argv)
294	    cli_values = vars(parsed)
295	    config_values = normalize_config(load_config(cli_values["config"])) if "config" in cli_values else {}
296	    merged = {**config_values, **cli_values}
297	    if not merged.get("out"):
298	        missing = []
299	        missing.append("--out")
300	        usage_error(f"artagents: missing required inputs: {', '.join(missing)}")
301	
302	    if not merged.get("brief") and not merged.get("plan"):
303	        usage_error("artagents: missing required inputs: --brief")
304	    if merged.get("brief") and merged.get("plan"):
305	        usage_error("artagents: pass only one of --brief or --plan")
306	    if merged.get("plan"):
307	        warnings.warn("--plan is deprecated; use --brief", DeprecationWarning)
308	        merged["brief"] = merged["plan"]
309	
310	    theme_explicit = "theme" in merged
311	    args = argparse.Namespace(**merged)
312	    args.theme_explicit = theme_explicit
313	    video_value = getattr(args, "video", None)
314	    args.video = (
315	        None
316	        if video_value is None
317	        else video_value if asset_cache.is_url(video_value) else Path(video_value).expanduser().resolve()
318	    )
319	    args.out = Path(args.out).expanduser().resolve()
320	    args.brief = Path(args.brief).expanduser().resolve()
```

```
927	    out: list[str] = []

932	            skip_next = False
```

```
103	    for command in plan.commands:

171	    except ValueError as exc:

173	    if not isinstance(manifest, dict):

183	def _dependency_install_command(executor: ExecutorDefinition, python_path: Path) -> tuple[str, ...] | None:
184	    pyproject_file = _metadata_path(executor, "pyproject_file")
185	    if pyproject_file is not None:

187	        return ("uv", "pip", "install", "--python", str(python_path), str(install_target))
188	
189	    requirements_file = _metadata_path(executor, "requirements_file")
190	    if requirements_file is not None:
191	        return ("uv", "pip", "install", "--python", str(python_path), "-r", str(requirements_file))
192	
193	    if executor.isolation.requirements:
194	        return ("uv", "pip", "install", "--python", str(python_path), *executor.isolation.requirements)
195	    return None

227	def _safe_manifest_source_dir(source: _GitManifestSource) -> Path:
228	    ref = source.commit_sha or source.tag or source.branch or source.source_ref or ""
229	    digest = sha256(f"{source.repo_url}\n{ref}\n{source.manifest_path}".encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:16]
230	    return Path(_safe_executor_id(source.expected_id)) / digest / "repo"

239	    ref = source.tag or source.branch or source.source_ref
240	    assert ref is not None
241	    _run_git(("git", "clone", "--depth", "1", "--branch", ref, source.repo_url, str(checkout)))
242
```

```
2	"""Element catalog facade over the ArtAgents elements registry."""
3	
4	from __future__ import annotations
5	
6	import os
7	import sys
8	from pathlib import Path
9	from typing import Any, Literal
10	
11	from artagents._paths import REPO_ROOT, WORKSPACE_ROOT
12	from artagents.elements.registry import ElementRegistry, ElementSource, load_default_registry, load_source_elements
13	from artagents.elements.schema import ELEMENT_KINDS, REQUIRED_ELEMENT_FILES

15	TOOLS_DIR = REPO_ROOT
16	THEMES_ROOT = WORKSPACE_ROOT / "themes"
17	
18	ElementKind = Literal["effects", "animations", "transitions"]
19	
20	
21	def _initial_active_theme() -> Path | None:
22	    raw = os.environ.get("HYPE_ACTIVE_THEME")
23	    if not raw:
24	        return None
25	    return _resolve_theme_dir(raw)
26	
27	
28	_ACTIVE_THEME_DIR: Path | None = None
29	
30	
31	def effects_root() -> Path:
32	    return element_root("effects")
33	
34	
35	def animations_root() -> Path:
36	    return element_root("animations")
37	
38	
39	def transitions_root() -> Path:
40	    return element_root("transitions")
41	
42	
43	def element_root(kind: ElementKind) -> Path:
44	    _validate_kind(kind)
45	    return WORKSPACE_ROOT / kind
46	
47	
48	def _validate_kind(kind: str) -> None:
49	    if kind not in ELEMENT_KINDS:
50	        raise ValueError(f"Invalid element kind {kind!r}")
51	
52	
53	def _singular(kind: ElementKind) -> str:
54	    return kind[:-1]
55	
56	
57	def _resolve_theme_dir(theme: str | Path | None) -> Path | None:
58	    if theme is None:
59	        return None
60	    candidate = Path(theme)
61	    if candidate.name == "theme.json":
62	        return candidate.parent.resolve()
63	    if candidate.exists():
64	        return (candidate if candidate.is_dir() else candidate.parent).resolve()
65	    return (THEMES_ROOT / str(theme)).resolve()
66	
67	
68	def set_active_theme(theme: str | Path | None) -> None:
69	    global _ACTIVE_THEME_DIR
70	    _ACTIVE_THEME_DIR = _resolve_theme_dir(theme)
71	
72	
73	_ACTIVE_THEME_DIR = _initial_active_theme()
74	
75	
76	def _active_theme_dir(theme: str | Path | None = None) -> Path | None:
77	    return _resolve_theme_dir(theme) if theme is not None else _ACTIVE_THEME_DIR
78	
79	
80	def _registry(theme: str | Path | None = None) -> ElementRegistry:
81	    theme_dir = _active_theme_dir(theme)
82	    registry = load_default_registry(active_theme=theme_dir, project_root=TOOLS_DIR)
83	    legacy_root = WORKSPACE_ROOT
84	    if legacy_root.exists():
85	        legacy_source = ElementSource("legacy_workspace", legacy_root, 15, True)
86	        for element in load_source_elements(legacy_source):
87	            registry.register(element)
88	    return registry
89	
90	
91	def _warn_conflicts(registry: ElementRegistry, *, kind: ElementKind) -> None:
92	    singular = _singular(kind)
93	    for conflict in registry.conflicts():
94	        if conflict.kind != kind:
95	            continue
96	        if conflict.winner.source == "active_theme":
97	            for shadowed in conflict.shadowed:
98	                if shadowed.source in {"legacy_workspace", "overrides", "managed", "bundled"}:
99	                    print(
100	                        f"WARN theme '{_theme_name_for_element(conflict.winner)}' overrides workspace {singular} '{conflict.id}'",
101	                        file=sys.stderr,
102	                    )
103	                    break
104	
105	
106	def _theme_name_for_element(element: Any) -> str:
107	    if element.root.parent.parent.name == "elements":
108	        return element.root.parent.parent.parent.name
109	    return element.root.parent.parent.name
110	
111	
112	def list_element_ids(kind: ElementKind, theme: str | Path | None = None) -> list[str]:
113	    _validate_kind(kind)
114	    registry = _registry(theme)
115	    _warn_conflicts(registry, kind=kind)
116	    return [element.id for element in registry.list(kind=kind)]
117	
118	
119	def _element(element_id: str, *, kind: ElementKind, theme: str | Path | None = None):
120	    _validate_kind(kind)
```

## COMPATIBILITY SHIM & MIGRATION DEBT: KILL LIST

**1. BARE-NAME ID RESOLUTION**
- **PURGE**: `executor_id_for_step()` at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/registry.py:95. Under packs, step names are already qualified; this helper is a compat shim that callers no longer need.
- **PURGE**: All hardcoded `"builtin.{step_name}"` string literals in `/artagents/orchestrators/hype/run.py:906`, `/artagents/executors/iteration_prepare/run.py:26,300,520` — derive from qualified IDs instead.

**2. ALIASING LAYERS**
- **PURGE**: `_resolve_alias()` at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/executors/understand/run.py:49. This aliases `audio` → `audio_understand`, `visual` → `visual_understand`, etc. Under packs, callers invoke the concrete executor directly; no dispatcher alias needed.
- **PURGE**: `build_steps()` at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/orchestrators/hype/run.py:921. It's a one-liner alias for `select_steps()` flagged "Backwards-compatible alias."

**3. SKIPPED/SPECIAL-CASED DIR NAMES**
- **PURGE**: Skip entry `"actions"` at /artagents/executors/registry.py:177. Directory never existed; historical remnant.
- **KEEP**: Skip entries `"builtin"`, `"bundled"`, `"curated"` at /artagents/executors/registry.py:177. These are structural (builtin is the step dir tree; bundled/curated are named discovery roots) — not compat.

**4. MODULE OVERRIDE MAP**
- **PURGE**: `_EXECUTOR_MODULE_OVERRIDES["render_remotion"]` at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/_paths.py:13–14. Exists only because old layout had `bin/render_remotion.py`; packs use canonical module names. Delete entire `_EXECUTOR_MODULE_OVERRIDES` dict and simplify `executor_argv()` to always use `f"artagents.executors.{stem}.run"`.

**5. DISCOVERY ROOT MULTIPLICITY**
- **KEEP**: `_builtin_folder_roots()`, `_bundled_folder_roots()`, `_project_folder_roots()` (executor/registry.py) and parallel orchestrator roots are load-bearing. Builtin = step-specific dirs; bundled/curated = named package dirs; project = external dirs. Three distinct concepts, not legacy.

**6. LEGACY/DEPRECATED COMMENTS**

> DEVELOPER

You are the autonomous megaplan runner for this single run.

Project: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents
Idea: see /tmp/megaplan-artagents-packs-idea.txt — read the file and pass its full contents as the idea string to `megaplan init`.
Execution mode: true (auto-approve)
Robustness: light

CRITICAL LAUNCHER NOTE: This system has a broken pyenv default. You MUST prefix every megaplan call with `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11`. Plain `python -m megaplan ...` fails. Use literally: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan <subcommand> ...` for every CLI call.

## 1. Role & Mission
Drive the megaplan workflow through the CLI until the run finishes or a defined breakpoint requires the outer conversation.

Always follow these priorities, in order:
1. The latest user direction relayed through notes or resume messages.
2. The live CLI state from `megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. The workflow and breakpoint rules in this template.
4. Your own memory of earlier turns.

Always do these things:
- Operate through the `megaplan` CLI only. Do not call workers or agents directly.
- Keep the outer conversation clean. Do not ask for routine confirmation.
- Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for routing. If memory and CLI state disagree, trust CLI state.
- Treat user notes as authoritative.

Never do these things:
- Do not run the workflow manually outside the CLI.
- Do not skip required phases for the selected robustness level.
- Do not emit a breakpoint unless one of the breakpoint rules below says to.

## 2. Startup
1. Read the idea file: `cat /tmp/megaplan-artagents-packs-idea.txt`
2. Note that `raw_config.execution.auto_approve` is explicitly true → expand `{AUTO_APPROVE_FLAG}` to empty string.
3. `raw_config.execution.robustness` is NOT explicitly set → expand `{ROBUSTNESS_FLAG}` to `--robustness light`.
4. Run: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan init --project-dir [REDACTED] --robustness light "$(cat /tmp/megaplan-artagents-packs-idea.txt)"`
5. Capture the returned plan name.
6. Output `PLAN_NAME: <name>` on its own line immediately after init and before any `BREAKPOINT:` or `COMPLETE:`.
7. Run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan <name>`.
8. From then on, use that plan name for every command.

After every status check:
- Read `state`, `next_step`, `valid_next`.
- If `notes_count > 0`, read the full `notes` array.
- Treat all notes as context. If the newest note changes direction, treat it as the new intent.

## 3. Phase Routing — Light Robustness
Light robustness loop: `init -> plan -> critique -> revise -> finalize -> execute -> done`
- There is NO prep, NO gate, NO review.
- After the light `revise`, the CLI moves to `gated`, so the next command is `finalize`.
- After `execute`, the CLI ends the run.

## 4. After Every Phase
Always run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan <name>` after each phase. Re-read `state`, `next_step`, `valid_next`. Trust CLI state over memory.

## 5. Breakpoints
Use a breakpoint only for the cases below. Format:
```
BREAKPOINT: <type>
Plan: <name>
State: <state>
Summary: <short reason>
Context: <artifacts, warnings, or the exact user decision needed>
```

Breakpoint types relevant at light robustness:
- `PHASE_ESCALATE` — a non-execute phase still fails after one --fresh retry, or returns unusable output twice.
- `EXECUTE_ESCALATE` — `execute` reaches the no-progress cap (3 consecutive attempts without forward progress), or repeated blocking/timeouts prevent progress.

## 6. Safeguards
Non-execute phases:
- If a phase fails, returns unusable output, or lands in a bad session state, retry once with `--fresh`.
- If still failing, stop with `BREAKPOINT: PHASE_ESCALATE`.

Execute:
- Count forward progress (new completed tasks, completed batch, state advance toward done).
- 3 consecutive `execute` attempts without forward progress → `BREAKPOINT: EXECUTE_ESCALATE`.

## 7. Resume Protocol
On resume:
1. Re-read the message.
2. Run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan <name>`.
3. Read full `notes` array.
4. Resume from CLI `next_step`, not memory.

## 8. Execution Details
After light `revise` (state becomes `gated`), run:
`PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan finalize --plan <name>`

In auto-approve mode, then run:
`PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive`

Per-batch:
- If execution briefing or response indicates per-batch, continue batch by batch.
- Use `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan progress --plan <name>` between batches.
- Re-run same batch number after a timeout; harness reconciles.

## 9. Overrides
- `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan override add-note --plan <name> --note "..."` — safe from any active state.
- `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan override force-proceed --plan <name> --reason "..."` — only if user clearly wants to override.
- `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan override abort --plan <name> --reason "..."` — only on explicit user request.

## 10. Completion Format
When done, return exactly:
```
COMPLETE: megaplan run finished
Plan: <name>
Final state: <state>
Summary: <outcome>
Artifacts: <key files or reports>
Follow-up: <only if something remains>
```

```
- artagents/packs/<pack-name>/<thing>/<manifest>.yaml — all registered content. A pack is any folder under packs/ with a pack.yaml. Each thing is a folder with a manifest file whose filename signals kind (executor.yaml / orchestrator.yaml / element.yaml).
- artagents/packs/builtin/ — the shipped pack containing all 33 executors + 4 orchestrators + 9 elements.
```

> DEVELOPER

You are a passive watcher for a long-running megaplan execution. Your only job is to poll plan status periodically and ping back when the run reaches a terminal state or needs human attention.

LAUNCHER: This system has a broken pyenv default. Prefix every megaplan call with `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11`. Plain `python -m megaplan ...` fails.

PLAN: `migrate-artagents-to-a-packs-20260503-2152`

POLL CYCLE:
1. Run: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan migrate-artagents-to-a-packs-20260503-2152`
2. Parse the JSON. Extract: `state`, `next_step`, `valid_next`, `active_step.health`, `active_step.age_seconds`, `active_step.recommended_action`, `notes_count`, `progress.tasks_done`, `progress.tasks_total`, `progress.batches_completed`, `progress.batches_total`.
3. Decide what to do:
   - If `state` is `done` (terminal success) → output COMPLETE block (see below) and stop.
   - If `state` is `aborted` or `error` → output BREAKPOINT block and stop.
   - If `valid_next` includes `override force-proceed`, `override abort`, or `override add-note` AS THE ONLY non-trivial choices (i.e. not just informational) → execution is stuck or escalated. Output BREAKPOINT block and stop.
   - If `active_step.health` is unhealthy or `recommended_action` is anything other than `wait`/`continue` → output BREAKPOINT block and stop.
   - If `state` is `executed` and `next_step` is `review`: at light robustness there is no review, so this should not occur. If it does, output BREAKPOINT block and stop.
   - If `next_step` is `execute` and `state` is `finalized` → execution still running, continue polling.
   - If progress hasn't advanced (`tasks_done` and `batches_completed` unchanged) for 3 consecutive polls AND `age_seconds` is approaching `escalation_threshold_seconds` → output BREAKPOINT block warning of stall.
4. Sleep until the next poll. Default poll interval: 600 seconds (10 minutes). If `recommended_next_check_seconds` is shorter, honor it. Use `sleep N` between polls.
5. Cap total runtime: if you've been polling for more than 3 hours total without resolution, output a BREAKPOINT block warning of long-running.

OUTPUT FORMATS:

When run reaches `done`:
```
COMPLETE: megaplan execution finished
Plan: migrate-artagents-to-a-packs-20260503-2152
Final state: <state>
Tasks: <done>/<total>
Batches: <completed>/<total>
Elapsed: <total polling time>
```

When something needs attention:
```
BREAKPOINT: <type>
Plan: migrate-artagents-to-a-packs-20260503-2152
State: <state>
Health: <health>
Tasks: <done>/<total>
Batches: <completed>/<total>
Reason: <one-sentence summary>
Recommended action: <what the user should consider>
```

DO NOT take any action yourself beyond polling status. Do not run execute, finalize, override, or any other workflow command. You are read-only.

Begin polling now. First poll immediately, then sleep and continue.

d=json.load(sys.stdin); p=d.get('progress') or {}; print(p.get('tasks_total',0))\" 2>/dev/null)\n  batches_completed=$(echo \"$out\" | python3 -c \"import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); p=d.get('progress') or {}; print(p.get('batches_completed',0))\" 2>/dev/null)\n  batches_total=$(echo \"$out\" | python3 -c \"import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); p=d.get('progress') or {}; print(p.get('batches_total',0))\" 2>/dev/null)\n  valid_next=$(echo \"$out\" | python3 -c \"import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(','.join(d.get('valid_next',[])))\" 2>/dev/null)\n\n  # Stall detection\n  if [ \"$tasks_done\" = \"$prev_tasks_done\" ] && [ \"$batches_completed\" = \"$prev_batches_completed\" ]; then\n    stall_count=$((stall_count+1))\n  else\n    stall_count=0\n  fi\n  prev_tasks_done=$tasks_done\n  prev_batches_completed=$batches_completed\n\n  # Terminal: done\n  if [ \"$state\" = \"done\" ]; then\n    echo \"TERMINAL_DONE poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s state=$state tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total\"\n    exit 0\n  fi\n  # Terminal: aborted/error\n  if [ \"$state\" = \"aborted\" ] || [ \"$state\" = \"error\" ]; then\n    echo \"TERMINAL_BAD poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s state=$state health=$health tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total reason=plan_${state}\"\n    exit 1\n  fi\n  # Health unhealthy or recommended_action not wait/continue\n  if [ \"$health\" != \"healthy\" ] && [ -n \"$health\" ]; then\n    if ! echo \"$valid_next\" | grep -qE \"execute|finalize|review|step|critique|plan\"; then\n      echo \"ATTENTION_STUCK poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s state=$state valid_next=$valid_next tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total\"\n      exit 4\n    fi\n  fi\n  # Unexpected executed+review combo\n  if [ \"$state\" = \"executed\" ] && [ \"$next_step\" = \"review\" ]; then\n    echo \"ATTENTION_REVIEW poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s state=$state next_step=$next_step tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total\"\n    exit 5\n  fi\n  # Stall warning\n  if [ \"$stall_count\" -ge 3 ] && [ \"$age\" -gt $((esc_thresh * 80 / 100)) ]; then\n    echo \"ATTENTION_STALL poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s state=$state age=${age}s esc_thresh=${esc_thresh}s stall_count=$stall_count tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total\"\n    exit 6\n  fi\n  # Long-running cap: 3 hours\n  if [ \"$elapsed\" -gt 10800 ]; then\n    echo \"ATTENTION_LONG poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s state=$state tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total\"\n    exit 7\n  fi\n\n  # Silent progress (no notification) — rotate to a log file readable later\n  echo \"[poll=$poll_num elapsed=${elapsed}s] state=$state next=$next_step health=$health age=${age}s tasks=$tasks_done/$tasks_total batches=$batches_completed/$batches_total notes=$notes valid_next=$valid_next\" >> /tmp/megaplan_watch_${PLAN}.log\n\n  # Sleep honoring recommended_next_check_seconds (min 60, cap 600)\n  sleep_s=$rec_check\n  if [ -z \"$sleep_s\" ] || [ \"$sleep_s\" -lt 60 ]; then sleep_s=300; fi\n  if [ \"$sleep_s\" -gt 600 ]; then sleep_s=600; fi\n  sleep \"$sleep_s\"\ndone"
```json
}
```

Robustness: light

CRITICAL LAUNCHER NOTE: This system has a broken pyenv default. You MUST prefix every megaplan call with `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11`. Plain `python -m megaplan ...` fails. Use literally: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan <subcommand> ...` for every CLI call.

PRIOR CONTEXT (this is a RESUME, not a fresh start)
- A prior plan migrate-artagents-to-a-packs-20260503-2152 was aborted because its codex worker died silently mid-batch-2.
- Tasks T1, T2, T3 from that plan are already complete on disk and committed (4160cfc + a594962).
- The new plan you'll create starts from a594962 and covers T4-T14 only.
- Test suite is currently green: 456 passed.

EXECUTION HINT FOR CODEX: when execute starts, codex should NOT redo T1-T3 work. The artagents/core/ namespace already exists with all framework files moved. Verify by running git log and checking the working tree before starting any task.

## 1. Role & Mission
Drive the megaplan workflow through the CLI until the run finishes or a defined breakpoint requires the outer conversation.

Always:
- Operate through the `megaplan` CLI only.
- Use `next_step` and `valid_next` for routing.
- Trust CLI state over memory.

Never:
- Skip required phases for light robustness.
- Emit a breakpoint unless one of the breakpoint rules below says to.

## 2. Startup
1. Read the idea file: `cat /tmp/megaplan-artagents-packs-resume.txt`
2. Run: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan init --project-dir [REDACTED] --robustness light "$(cat /tmp/megaplan-artagents-packs-resume.txt)"`
3. Capture the returned plan name.
4. Output `PLAN_NAME: <name>` on its own line immediately.
5. Run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan <name>`.
6. Continue with phase routing.

## 3. Phase Routing — Light Robustness
Loop: `init -> plan -> critique -> revise -> finalize -> execute -> done`
- No prep, no gate, no review.
- After light `revise`, state becomes `gated`; next is `finalize`.
- After `execute`, run ends.

## 4. After Every Phase
Run `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan <name>` after each phase.

## 5. Execute — IMPORTANT: USE PER-BATCH MODE
Instead of running a single `megaplan execute --confirm-destructive`, drive execution batch by batch so a worker death between batches doesn't strand state.

For each batch N:
1. Run: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan execute --plan <name> --confirm-destructive --batch N`
2. After it returns, run: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan progress --plan <name>` to confirm the batch landed.
3. Run: `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan <name>` to check overall state.
4. If state is `done`, stop and emit COMPLETE.
5. Otherwise proceed to batch N+1.

The harness validates that batches 1..N-1 are complete before running batch N. The final batch transitions state to `executed` (in light robustness this is the terminal state since there is no review).

## 6. Breakpoints
Use a breakpoint only for the cases below. Format:
```
BREAKPOINT: <type>
Plan: <name>
State: <state>
Summary: <short reason>
Context: <decision needed>
```

- `PHASE_ESCALATE` — non-execute phase fails after one --fresh retry, or returns unusable output twice.
- `EXECUTE_ESCALATE` — same `--batch N` call returns no forward progress 3 times in a row, or repeated blocking/timeouts prevent progress.

## 7. Safeguards
- Non-execute phase failure: retry once with `--fresh`. If still failing, BREAKPOINT.
- Execute batch N failure: retry the same batch up to 3 times. If still no progress, BREAKPOINT.
- Total runtime cap: 4 hours. If exceeded, BREAKPOINT.

## 8. Completion Format
When `state` is `done` or `executed` (terminal at light), output exactly:
```
COMPLETE: megaplan run finished
Plan: <name>
Final state: <state>
Tasks: <done>/<total>
Batches: <completed>/<total>
Summary: <outcome>
```

Begin now. Run init.

> DEVELOPER

Fix a bug in the megaplan CLI tool. The source is at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/.

THE BUG
`megaplan progress --plan <name>` and `megaplan status --plan <name>` both return zeros for task and batch counters EVEN WHEN the harness has actually completed batches and written batch artifacts on disk.

CONCRETE REPRO
- Plan name: `resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311`
- Plan dir: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/.megaplan/plans/resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311/`
- Real state on disk:
  - `execution_batch_1.json` exists (1790 bytes, written 23:29:46) — proves batch 1 completed
  - Final state: there's also a commit landed in the user repo for T4 (the task in batch 1)
  - The plan has 12 tasks across 12 batches per the finalize output
- What megaplan reports:
  - `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan progress --plan resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311` returns: `summary: ''`, `tasks: done=0 pending=0 total=0`, `batches: completed=0 total=0`, empty tasks array
  - `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311` returns `progress.tasks_done=0`, `progress.tasks_total=0`, `progress.batches_completed=0` etc.
- Both should reflect 1/12 tasks done, 1/12 batches completed.

LIKELY CULPRIT
The progress aggregation function probably reads from `execution.json` (the aggregate, written only after the final batch) instead of summing `execution_batch_*.json` files when they exist. So in per-batch mode, until the last batch lands and the aggregate is written, progress reports zero. This would be a known weakness when running per-batch — the docs even say "Progress reflects the last finalize.json write (between-batch granularity)."

But the user is running per-batch precisely to get batch-level granularity, and the comment in docs ("With per-batch mode, each batch is a separate CLI call, so the orchestrator has full visibility") suggests per-batch mode SHOULD give better granularity. The reporting code may simply not be reading the per-batch artifacts.

YOUR JOB
1. Find the progress-reporting code path. Likely `megaplan/progress.py`, `megaplan/cli.py`, or wherever the `progress` subcommand is implemented. Also find what produces the `progress` field in `status` responses.
2. Confirm whether the bug is "doesn't read execution_batch_*.json" or something else. Don't guess — verify by reading the code.
3. Fix it: when per-batch artifacts exist, aggregate from them. Specifically, walk `execution_batch_*.json` in the plan dir, sum the completed tasks from each, and report that. Fall back to `execution.json` if no per-batch artifacts exist (old single-execute mode).
4. Make sure the fix preserves the ordering / batch number derivation correctly.
5. Verify by running `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan progress --plan resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311` and `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311` after your fix. Both should now report 1/12 tasks, 1/12 batches.
6. Don't introduce new tests unless there are obvious existing tests in `megaplan/tests/` for the progress code that you should update. Don't restructure surrounding code.

LAUNCHER
This system has a broken pyenv default (Python 3.8.10 set globally but not installed). All megaplan CLI invocations need `PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11` prefix:
`PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan <subcommand>`

PROJECT CONVENTIONS
- Megaplan is a Python project. Check for `pyproject.toml` to understand how it's invoked.
- The repo likely has its own test convention. Look at `megaplan/tests/` if you make code changes that warrant a test update.
- DO NOT break existing single-execute mode (where `execution.json` is the only artifact).
- Keep the fix minimal — this is a single-bug fix, not a refactor.

Report back: the file you changed, the diff, your verification command output, and whether existing tests still pass.

```
155	def _build_progress_payload(plan_dir: Path, state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
156	    finalize_path = plan_dir / "finalize.json"
157	    if not finalize_path.exists():
158	        return {
159	            "summary": "No finalize.json yet — plan has not been finalized.",
160	            "tasks_total": 0,
161	            "tasks_done": 0,
162	            "tasks_skipped": 0,
163	            "tasks_pending": 0,
164	            "tasks_blocked": 0,
165	            "batches_total": 0,
166	            "batches_completed": 0,
167	            "tasks": [],
168	        }
169	    finalize_data = read_json(finalize_path)
170	    global_batches = compute_global_batches(finalize_data)
171	    tasks = finalize_data.get("tasks", [])
172	    task_id_to_batch: dict[str, int] = {}
173	    for batch_idx, batch_ids in enumerate(global_batches, start=1):
174	        for task_id in batch_ids:
175	            task_id_to_batch[task_id] = batch_idx
176	    tasks_done = sum(1 for t in tasks if t.get("status") == "done")
177	    tasks_skipped = sum(1 for t in tasks if t.get("status") == "skipped")
178	    tasks_pending = sum(1 for t in tasks if t.get("status") == "pending")
179	    tasks_blocked = sum(1 for t in tasks if t.get("status") == "blocked")
180	    tasks_total = len(tasks)
181	    completed_ids = {
182	        t["id"] for t in tasks if t.get("status") in {"done", "skipped"} and isinstance(t.get("id"), str)
183	    }
184	    batches_completed = sum(
185	        1
186	        for batch_ids in global_batches
187	        if all(tid in completed_ids for tid in batch_ids)
188	    )
189	    task_status_list = [
190	        {
191	            "id": t.get("id", ""),
192	            "status": t.get("status", "pending"),
193	            "batch": task_id_to_batch.get(t.get("id", ""), 0),
194	        }
195	        for t in tasks
196	    ]
197	    return {
198	        "summary": (
199	            f"Execution progress: {tasks_done + tasks_skipped}/{tasks_total} tasks tracked, "
200	            f"{batches_completed}/{len(global_batches)} batches completed. "
201	            "Progress reflects the last finalize.json write (between-batch granularity)."
202	        ),
203	        "tasks_total": tasks_total,
204	        "tasks_done": tasks_done,
205	        "tasks_skipped": tasks_skipped,
206	        "tasks_pending": tasks_pending,
207	        "tasks_blocked": tasks_blocked,
208	        "batches_total": len(global_batches),
209	        "batches_completed": batches_completed,
210	        "tasks": task_status_list,
211	    }

214	def _build_last_step(state: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:

230	def _build_active_step(active_step: Any, *, plan_dir: Path) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
231	    if not isinstance(active_step, dict):
232	        return None
233	    details = dict(active_step)
234	    step = details.get("step")
235	    if not isinstance(step, str) or not step:
236	        return details
237	    configured_timeout_seconds = int(get_effective("execution", "worker_timeout_seconds"))
238	    lock_held = plan_lock_is_held(plan_dir)
239	    started_at = _parse_utc_timestamp(details.get("started_at"))
240	    if started_at is not None:
241	        age_seconds = max(0, int((datetime.now(timezone.utc) - started_at).total_seconds()))
242	        details.update(
243	            build_phase_observability(
244	                step,
245	                configured_timeout_seconds=configured_timeout_seconds,
246	                age_seconds=age_seconds,
247	                lock_held=lock_held,
248	            )
249	        )
250	        if details.get("stale"):
251	            orphaned = not lock_held
252	            details["orphaned"] = orphaned
253	            if orphaned:
254	                if step == "execute":
255	                    details["recovery_hint"] = (
256	                        "The active step is stale and no process holds the plan lock. "
257	                        "Safe next action: rerun the same execute command on Codex without --fresh."
258	                    )
259	                else:
260	                    details["recovery_hint"] = (
261	                        "The active step is stale and no process holds the plan lock. "
262	                        "Safe next action: rerun the same step on the same agent before escalating."
263	                    )
264	        max_seconds = int(details.get("expected_duration_seconds", {}).get("max", 0) or 0)
265	        elapsed_label = humanize_seconds(age_seconds)
266	        if details.get("stale"):
267	            details["phase_progress_summary"] = (
268	                f"{step} stale ({elapsed_label} elapsed, expected max {humanize_seconds(max_seconds)}) "
269	                "see recovery_hint."
270	            )
271	        elif step in {"execute", "loop_execute"}:
272	            details["phase_progress_summary"] = (
273	                f"{step} running ({elapsed_label} elapsed, use progress for batch-level detail)."
274	            )
275	        else:
276	            details["phase_progress_summary"] = (
277	                f"{step} running ({elapsed_label} elapsed, typically completes within "
278	                f"{humanize_seconds(max_seconds)})."
279	            )
280	            if max_seconds > 0:
281	                details["progress_pct"] = min(95, int((age_seconds / max_seconds) * 100))
282	    else:
283	        details.update(

286	                configured_timeout_seconds=configured_timeout_seconds,

294	        else:
295	            details["phase_progress_summary"] = f"{step} active (start time unknown)."
296	    return details
```

```
122	def fsync_dir(path: Path) -> None:
123	    directory = path if path.is_dir() else path.parent
124	    directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
125	    fd = os.open(directory, os.O_RDONLY)
126	    try:
127	        _fsync_file_descriptor(fd)
128	    finally:
129	        os.close(fd)
```

```
862	def batch_artifact_path(plan_dir: Path, batch_number: int) -> Path:

866	def list_batch_artifacts(plan_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:
867	    def sort_key(path: Path) -> tuple[int, str]:
868	        match = re.fullmatch(r"execution_batch_(\d+)\.json", path.name)
869	        if match is None:
870	            raise ValueError(f"Unexpected batch artifact filename: {path.name}")
871	        return (int(match.group(1)), path.name)
872	
873	    return sorted(
874	        (
875	            path
876	            for path in plan_dir.glob("execution_batch_*.json")
877	            if path.is_file() and re.fullmatch(r"execution_batch_(\d+)\.json", path.name)
878	        ),
879	        key=sort_key,
880	    )
881	
882	
883	def current_iteration_artifact(plan_dir: Path, prefix: str, iteration: int) -> Path:
884	    return plan_dir / f"{prefix}_v{iteration}.json"
885	
886	
887	def current_iteration_raw_artifact(plan_dir: Path, prefix: str, iteration: int) -> Path:
```

Now I have everything I need to write the fix. The plan:

1. After loading `finalize_data`, check for `execution_batch_*.json` files.
2. If they exist, build a `task_id -> status` overlay from their `task_updates`.
3. Apply that overlay to the tasks (overriding finalize.json's stale status) when computing counts.

This preserves single-execute mode (no batch artifacts → use finalize.json as today) and adds per-batch progress tracking.

Let me also confirm: in single-execute mode there's no `execution_batch_*.json`, and the final `execution.json` is presumably written and updates state. Let me check what writes back to finalize.json.

> AGENT

So `finalize.json` is only updated AFTER all execution completes (single-execute mode), or in per-batch mode it is only updated at the very end. In per-batch mode, between batches the `finalize.json` task statuses remain "pending" and only `execution_batch_*.json` files reflect progress. Confirmed bug.

## Report

**File changed:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cli.py`

**Bug confirmed:** `_build_progress_payload` only read task statuses from `finalize.json`. In single-execute mode that's fine — `finalize.json` is rewritten at the end. In per-batch mode the executor writes one `execution_batch_<n>.json` per batch but does not rewrite `finalize.json` until the final batch completes, so all in-progress task statuses stayed `pending` and all counters returned 0.

**Fix:** `_build_progress_payload` now walks `execution_batch_*.json` files (via the existing `list_batch_artifacts` helper from `megaplan._core`), collects `task_id -> status` from each file's `task_updates`, and overlays those statuses onto the tasks loaded from `finalize.json` before counting. Per-batch ordering is preserved because `list_batch_artifacts` returns artifacts sorted by numeric batch index, so later batches correctly overwrite earlier ones for the same task. When no batch artifacts exist (single-execute mode, or pre-execute), the overlay is empty and behavior is unchanged. The progress summary's "granularity_note" suffix now also reports which source was used.

**Diff (logical):**
- Added `list_batch_artifacts` to the existing `from megaplan._core import (...)` block.
- Inside `_build_progress_payload`, after loading `finalize_data`, build `batch_status_overlay` from `execution_batch_*.json` task_updates; if non-empty, replace each task's `status` with the overlay value before computing `tasks_done/skipped/pending/blocked` and `batches_completed`. Summary text now picks one of two granularity notes depending on whether any batch artifact was found.

**Verification:**

`PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan progress --plan resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311` now reports:
```
"summary": "Execution progress: 1/12 tasks tracked, 1/12 batches completed. Progress reflects per-batch artifacts (latest execution_batch_*.json overlay)."
"tasks_total": 12, "tasks_done": 1, "tasks_pending": 11,
"batches_total": 12, "batches_completed": 1
```
with `T4` listed as `done` in batch 1 and the rest still `pending`.

`PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 megaplan status --plan resume-the-artagents-packs-20260503-2311` shows the same progress block under `response["progress"]`, plus the matching summary line.

**Tests:** All 74 tests in `tests/test_init_plan.py`, `tests/test_cli_entry.py`, `tests/test_core.py` pass — including the seven existing `progress`/`watch` tests (`test_progress_all_pending`, `test_progress_after_partial_execution`, `test_progress_after_full_execution`, `test_handle_status_includes_progress_when_finalize_exists`, `test_handle_watch_combines_status_and_progress`, etc.). The single-execute path (no batch artifacts → falls back to finalize.json) is the path those existing tests exercise, so backward compatibility is verified. No new tests added per instructions.
